#### Abstracts
##### **SMI: Ontology-Grounded Semantic IDs for Hierarchy-Aware Medical Concept Representation**
2610.18672v1 by Ziyang Song, Lia Shen, Yixuan Li, Qincheng Lu, Weihao Li, Haoran Xu, He Zhu, Xinyu Wang, Yue Li

Biomedical language models (LMs) provide strong representations for clinical tasks, but they often fail to capture the hierarchical structure among medical concepts. This limitation is especially pronounced for rare and fine-grained clinical codes in long-tailed vocabularies, where hierarchy-agnostic LM representations become less informative as concept frequency decreases. We introduce Semantic Medical Identifier (SMI), a training-free framework for constructing ontology-grounded Semantic IDs and hierarchy-aware medical concept representations. For each diagnosis, procedure, or medication concept, SMI defines its Semantic ID as the coarse-to-fine path through an expert-defined medical ontology. SMI then aggregates frozen biomedical LM embeddings across the corresponding ontology levels to form hierarchy-aware medical concept representations that preserve coarse-to-fine clinical semantics. The base SMI uses uniform aggregation across ontology levels, while a level-weighted variant selects global level weights on validation data. Our theoretical analysis shows that SMI yields more informative medical concept representations than hierarchy-agnostic LM embeddings, particularly for rare and fine-grained clinical concepts. Experiments show that SMI captures meaningful hierarchical structure and improves clinical relation prediction. On MIMIC-IV, SMI improves mortality prediction, readmission prediction, drug recommendation, and rare drug prediction. On eICU, SMI yields more robust patient representations under cross-hospital distribution shift.

摘要：

##### **Geometric Calibration of Supervised Fine-Tuning**
2610.18634v1 by Jianzhu Bao, Jia-Chen Gu, Qingyuan Liu, Haozhen Zhang, Kai-Wei Chang, Nanyun Peng, Wenya Wang

Recent studies investigate the weight geometry through its spectral structure, which characterizes how the weights transform different input directions. Within this view, supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is shown to induce dense updates that perturb the spectral geometry of pre-SFT weights along their principal directions, potentially disrupting the model's prior knowledge. These observations motivate using the geometry of pre-SFT weights as a reference for refining learned SFT updates. We study this perspective as Geometric Calibration and develop SPACE Subspace Projection for Adapted Checkpoint Enhancement), a lightweight post-hoc method that selectively reverts SFT update components within the principal subspace of the pre-SFT weights while preserving those in its orthogonal complement. SPACE is a data-free, training-free method that requires only the pre- and post-SFT checkpoints. We evaluate SPACE on Qwen2.5, Qwen3, Qwen3.5, Llama-3.1, and Olmo-3 models ranging from 2B to 14B, across mathematical, coding, and medical SFT, as well as several SFT variants. Overall, SPACE mitigates out-of-domain (OOD) capability regression while further improving in-domain (ID) performance over the uncalibrated SFT checkpoints. For math SFT, SPACE yields average ID/OOD gains of 2.85/2.26 points on Qwen2.5-7B, and 2.41/1.52 points on Qwen3-14B-Base, respectively. These results highlight how the geometric relationship between SFT updates and pre-SFT weights shapes the adapted model's capabilities and, more broadly, motivate geometry-aware parameter updates for continual model improvement.

摘要：

##### **Task-evoked eye movements as candidate digital biomarkers of depression severity in adolescents and young adults**
2610.18494v1 by Youxiang Sun, Yujie Nie, Zhuoru Wu, Mengyuan Yu, Wenkong Wang, Xiang Li, Yumei Wang, Xin Ma, Lizhou Fan

Objective digital measures of depression severity could complement conventional symptom assessments and facilitate more timely clinical evaluation. Existing monitoring methods typically control false alarms by requiring the discrepancy measure used to detect shifts to be *pre-specified*. However, for unstructured data, pre-specification creates a sensitivity--breadth trade-off: a narrowly defined measure is sensitive to the anticipated shift but may miss unexpected changes, whereas a broadly defined measure may detect a wider range of shifts but with reduced sensitivity. Although adaptively hypothesizing the shift direction can overcome this limitation, such data-dependent adaptation generally invalidates conventional false-alarm guarantees. We introduce *asymptotic e-detectors*, which approximate the pre-change distribution using a calibration set of size $m$, and prove that they asymptotically control the average run length to a false alarm as $m$ increases. Empirically, calibration sets as small as $m=30$ provide effective control. We then develop adaptive monitoring strategies ranging from black-box bettors that learn from embeddings to interpretable bettors based on LLM-proposed concepts. Across three real-world datasets, including data from a clinical AI scribe deployment, adaptive monitors detect shifts faster than fixed monitors at the same false-alarm level. Further, when the true shifts are sparse and interpretable, LLM-concept monitors provide the fastest and most interpretable detection, while also guiding root-cause diagnosis for complex shifts.

We propose ARMED, an adaptive clinical RAG framework that dynamically expands retrieval according to retrieval sufficiency and current-admission relevance. ARMED first observes the initial retrieval outcome and selectively performs supplementary or fallback multi-query retrieval when additional evidence is needed. The framework also incorporates a relevance gate that activates only clinically relevant same-patient history and organizes retrieved evidence into structured query-level clinical evidence. Experiments on MIMIC-IV demonstrate that ARMED consistently outperforms previous static retrieval baselines and improves overall medication recommendation quality. By combining locally runnable small language models with adaptive retrieval, ARMED supports medication recommendation without relying on external language model APIs or task-specific fine-tuning.

Automating RWS with large language model agents is appealing but brittle: valid analyses depend on dataset-specific clinical semantics, including index dates, follow-up windows, patient/event-level units, and outcome boundaries that are often implicit in observational data. ##### **MontageFlow: Physically Valid High Fidelity EEG Generation**

Although recent generative models report substantial improvements in generation fidelity, we show that these gains conceal a categorical failure. EEG is not an arbitrary multichannel signal: each channel records the voltage difference between two scalp electrodes. So, real recordings satisfy exact linear identities among their channels. Current generators invariably violate these identities, yet this has gone unnoticed. We therefore propose MontageFlow, a flow-matching model that enforces these identities by restricting its output to signals that satisfy them. We find that ensuring validity improves fidelity too. Furthermore, to retain the EEG characteristics that oversmoothing washes out, we also condition the model on a physiological state vector encoded from the whole EEG window. For generation, this vector is drawn from a prior fitted in latent space. Across 3 large-scale benchmarks, our model improves TS-FID by 1.6-3.6$\times$ over the closest rival while ensuring validity.

Guided by a progression-aware state representation and a diversity-informed reward, the agent fluidly reallocates computational resources between the differencevector-based exploration of Differential Evolution (DE) and the covariance-guided exploitation of CMA-ES, while jointly regulating population size, elite preservation, and a restart mechanism to escape local optima. A rhythm-stratified and point-wise analysis further characterizes the distinctive behavior of each tool, yielding practical guidance for tool selection. These results provide systematic, multi-dataset evidence that self-supervised pretraining is effective for ECG delineation and enables a label-efficiently trained deep learning model to outperform widely used delineation tools by leveraging abundant unlabeled data. This supports adopting such models in diverse, real-world clinical settings.

摘要：心電圖（ECG）輪廓描繪，即波形邊界的識別，是將原始ECG信號轉換為臨床可解釋測量的基礎步驟。深度學習已經推進了這項任務，但仍然依賴於昂貴的專家標註。預期標籤高效策略，如自監督預訓練和半監督學習，能夠減輕這一負擔，但仍不清楚它們是否能產生可靠的輪廓描繪，以及它們所生成的深度模型是否優於實際使用的輪廓描繪工具。我們將此問題分為兩個階段來解決。首先，通過在一個內部數據集和四個外部數據集上比較自監督目標與監督或半監督微調，我們發現預訓練有幫助，但目標很重要，且半監督微調的價值取決於預訓練目標。其次，我們使用三個互補的指標，將所選的深度學習模型與廣泛使用的開源（NeuroKit2、Prominence、ECGdeli）和商業（CalECG）工具進行基準測試。該模型在每個指標和數據集上均排名最佳，在節律多樣的數據集上明顯超越最強工具（mIoU 71.3 vs. 54.8%；平均逐點靈敏度 92.6 vs. 76.4%），並且從竇性心律到心律不整的降級最小。對節律分層和逐點分析進一步描述了每個工具的獨特行為，為工具選擇提供了實用指導。這些結果提供了系統的多數據集證據，表明自監督預訓練對ECG輪廓描繪是有效的，並使得標籤高效訓練的深度學習模型能夠通過利用豐富的未標記數據超越廣泛使用的輪廓描繪工具。這支持在多樣的現實臨床環境中採用這類模型。

##### **Novice Reliance Calibration in AI-Assisted Decision Making: The Role of Explanations and Self-Assessment**
2610.07800v1 by Eun Jeong Kang,  Peter,  Duan, Swati Mishra

Artificial Intelligence (AI) tools are widely used to support decision making in tasks and domains where no immediate performance feedback is available. In these settings, users cannot learn to adjust their reliance behavior over time through trial and error. However, little is known about how novice users calibrate reliance on AI when external feedback is unavailable, or whether AI explanations can support calibration in its absence. We introduce reliance calibration as an organizing construct for studying how novice users dynamically adjust reliance behavior, and examine how AI explanations and meta-cognitive self-assessment shape it. Through a between-subjects study with 110 participants completing a clinical entity extraction task with AI assistance and limited performance feedback, we observe that novice users exhibit systematic drift toward over-reliance in the presence of explanations, while higher self-reported task understanding is associated with more selective reliance behavior. These results extend reliance calibration research into human-AI collaboration contexts without real-time performance signals and present actionable guidelines on designing AI tools that must support appropriate reliance in these settings.

摘要：人工智慧（AI）工具被廣泛用於支持在沒有即時表現反饋的任務和領域中的決策制定。<lb/>在這些情況下，用戶無法通過反覆試驗來學習隨時間調整其依賴行為。<lb/>然而，對於新手用戶在缺乏外部反饋的情況下如何調整對AI的依賴，或者AI解釋是否能在缺乏反饋的情況下支持這種調整，了解的資訊卻很少。<lb/>我們引入依賴調整作為一個組織性構念，用於研究新手用戶如何動態調整依賴行為，並檢視AI解釋和元認知自我評估如何影響這一過程。<lb/>通過一項包含110名參與者的被試間研究，這些參與者在AI輔助下完成一項臨床實體提取任務，並且獲得有限的表現反饋，我們觀察到新手用戶在有解釋的情況下表現出系統性的過度依賴趨勢，而自我報告的任務理解程度較高則與更具選擇性的依賴行為相關。<lb/>這些結果將依賴調整研究擴展到沒有實時表現信號的人機協作情境，並提供了設計必須在這些情境中支持適當依賴的AI工具的可行指導方針。

##### **Unanimously Wrong: Certified Abstention from How Medical LLM Consensus Forms**
2610.07570v1 by Xiaoyang Wang, Tianrui Wang, Christopher C. Yang

In clinical practice, agreement among independent experts is treated as evidence of reliability, and multi-round consensus has become a core mechanism of agentic medical question-answering systems. When such a system must decide whether to trust its own answer, the prevailing signal is again agreement, now among the sampled answers. But agreement is a fragile proxy for correctness. A system can be unanimously wrong, returning the same incorrect answer on every sample, and on these questions agreement-based signals carry no information. The cause is that these signals read only the final state of the consensus and discard how it was reached. Agreement that was reached by resolving disagreement with evidence looks identical, at the end, to agreement that was present from the first sample because every sample shares one misconception. ProbeGuard is a certified abstention framework that bases the abstention decision on how the consensus formed. Process features trace agreement trajectories, minority persistence, and retrieval saturation. For unanimous votes, rationale semantic entropy checks whether the reasons behind the vote cohere, and an active probe retrieves counter-evidence and measures whether the consensus survives. A stratified Learn-then-Test calibration then converts these scores into a distribution-free bound on selective risk. We evaluate ProbeGuard on three medical QA benchmarks and a hard-frontier reference, with a published multi-round agentic RAG substrate, against six abstention baselines. On MedQA, 13.4% of unanimous votes are wrong, and no agreement-based signal can flag them. Process signals raise the discrimination of correct from incorrect consensus from chance to 0.696 AUROC. The certified rule answers six in ten unanimous-layer questions at an observed selective risk of 9.0%, and nine in ten once in-domain calibration data accumulate.

摘要：在臨床實踐中，獨立專家的一致意見被視為可靠性的證據，而多輪共識已成為代理醫療問答系統的核心機制。當這樣的系統必須決定是否信任自己的答案時，主要的信號再次是一致性，這次是在抽樣的答案之間。然而，一致性是一個脆弱的正確性代理。系統可以在所有樣本中一致地錯誤，返回相同的不正確答案，而在這些問題上，基於一致性的信號並不提供任何信息。原因在於這些信號僅僅讀取共識的最終狀態，並忽略了它是如何達成的。通過用證據解決分歧達成的一致性，在最後看起來與從第一個樣本開始就存在的一致性是相同的，因為每個樣本都共享一個誤解。ProbeGuard是一個經過認證的放棄框架，基於共識形成的方式來決定是否放棄。過程特徵追蹤一致性軌跡、少數持續性和檢索飽和度。對於一致的投票，理由語義熵檢查投票背後的理由是否一致，而主動探測則檢索反證據並測量共識是否持續。分層的先學後測校準然後將這些分數轉換為無分佈的選擇風險界限。我們在三個醫療QA基準和一個困難邊界參考上評估ProbeGuard，並與六個放棄基準進行比較，這些基準有一個已發表的多輪代理RAG基礎。在MedQA上，13.4%的全體投票是錯誤的，並且沒有基於一致性的信號能夠標記它們。過程信號將正確與不正確共識的區分從偶然提升到0.696 AUROC。經過認證的規則在觀察到的選擇風險為9.0%時，回答了十分之一的一致層問題，而當領域內的校準數據累積後，則回答了十分之九。

##### **PsyCIDRA: A Dual-Agent Framework for Psychiatric Interviewing and Diagnostic Reasoning**
2610.07473v1 by Milad Mohammadi, Fatemeh Akrami Shamsabadi, Zahra Mohseni, Amirhossein Safdarian, Malekfarhad Malek, Hadi Moradi, Hesham Faili

Large language models show promise in clinical reasoning, but psychiatric interviewing requires guiding an evolving conversation. Their ability to carry out this interactive assessment remains less studied. We present PsyCIDRA, a dual-agent framework linking free-form psychiatric interviewing with diagnostic reasoning for expert review. Its interviewer agent uses tools to maintain working notes, load expert-written skills, and retrieve ICD-11 references to guide inquiry. Its diagnostic reasoning agent then receives the completed interview transcript and reports hypotheses alongside supporting, conflicting, and missing evidence, withholding a final hypothesis when none is sufficiently supported. Using patient profiles generated with PsyCPG, we first evaluate PsyCIDRA in simulation. Across four models on 53 evaluation cases, it achieves higher diagnostic agreement than direct prompting. On 81 held-out simulated cases, rank-1 accuracy is 60.5% versus 51.9%. In a blinded study of 101 human participants in separate arms, PsyCIDRA agrees with psychologists on whether to propose a diagnostic hypothesis in 79.6% of cases, compared with 65.4% for direct prompting. Together, these findings support the potential of LLM agents to assist psychiatric assessment through free-form dialogue. By examining diagnostic reasoning, interview quality, and safety together, this study contributes to understanding the capabilities and limitations of psychiatric interview agents.

摘要：大型語言模型在臨床推理中顯示出潛力，但精神病訪談需要引導一個不斷演變的對話。它們進行這種互動評估的能力仍然研究較少。我們提出了PsyCIDRA，一個雙代理框架，將自由形式的精神病訪談與專家審查的診斷推理聯繫起來。其訪談代理使用工具來維持工作筆記、加載專家撰寫的技能，並檢索ICD-11參考資料以指導詢問。然後，它的診斷推理代理接收完成的訪談文字記錄，並報告假設以及支持的、相互矛盾的和缺失的證據，當沒有任何證據足夠支持時，則不提出最終假設。使用PsyCPG生成的患者檔案，我們首先在模擬中評估PsyCIDRA。在53個評估案例的四個模型中，它達到的診斷一致性高於直接提示。在81個保留的模擬案例中，排名第一的準確率為60.5%，而直接提示為51.9%。在對101名人類參與者進行的盲測研究中，PsyCIDRA在79.6%的案例中與心理學家一致是否提出診斷假設，而直接提示則為65.4%。這些發現共同支持LLM代理通過自由形式對話協助精神病評估的潛力。通過共同檢查診斷推理、訪談質量和安全性，本研究有助於理解精神病訪談代理的能力和限制。

##### **Adaptive Gait Biofeedback With Participant-Held-Out Modeling and Participant-Specific Updating in Chronic Ankle Instability**
2610.07428v2 by Jaeyoon Kim, Veronika Lebisova, Jeniya Sultana, Jaeyoung Cho, Jaeho Jang

Adaptive gait biofeedback may support repeated practice in chronic ankle instability, but its evaluation must address model performance and human response. We evaluated a temporal convolutional classifier on protocol-defined, angle-derived GOOD/BAD gait-cycle labels using participant-held-out leave-one-subject-out (LOSO) cross-validation in 20 participants. Seven participants in the adaptive-intervention group completed nine sessions over three weeks, with one motion-capture recording analyzed per session. Models updated after failed sessions were compared offline with their parent models on the same-session validation subset used for candidate selection and the first subsequent adaptive-session recording. Frontal-plane ankle angle was compared between the adaptive group and 10 sequentially enrolled controls at Baseline, Post, and 7-day Retention. Across 20 held-out folds, mean fold-level area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) was 0.948, sensitivity for angle-threshold-exceeding BAD cycles was 0.941, and specificity for angle-threshold-meeting GOOD cycles was 0.366. Mean BAD-class F1 was higher in candidate models by 0.187 on the same-session subset and 0.118 on the first subsequent recording. At Post, the adaptive group had a baseline-adjusted frontal-plane ankle angle 5.168 degrees lower than controls (95% confidence interval, 1.766-8.569 degrees lower); the Retention contrast was uncertain. These findings characterize population-model discrimination and offline participant-specific updating during repeated biofeedback use, alongside a nonrandomized Post frontal-plane ankle angle association. They do not establish independent clinical gait classification or a causal benefit of updating.

#### Abstracts
##### **Does self-distillation improve reasoning?**
2610.18613v1 by Jason Ken Adhinarta, Raina Wu, Ashutosh Sharma, Avinash Balakrishnan, Rameswar Panda, Polina Golland, Yoon Kim, Zhang-Wei Hong

Self-distillation is an emerging approach that trains a model to imitate its own predictions when conditioned on privileged information, such as correct answers or environmental feedback. Recent methods have reported better sample efficiency and generalization than reinforcement learning (RL), but it remains unclear whether these gains extend beyond in-distribution evaluations and whether they stem from privileged distillation. In this paper, we revisit two representative methods, Self-Distillation Policy Optimization (SDPO) and On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD), across code, science, tool use, and math reasoning tasks. We find that their reported gains do not consistently generalize to unseen problems and are largely explained by overfitting or transferring existing reasoning behavior, rather than effectively using privileged information. Moreover, self-distillation gradients show weak alignment with policy gradients that directly optimize expected reward, questioning whether they provide an effective direction for policy improvement. Our experiments across Qwen3 models (1.7B-14B) and OLMo-3-7B suggest that effectively leveraging privileged information to improve reasoning remains an open challenge. Our code is available at https://github.com/sleepypandu/does-sd-improve-reasoning

摘要：

##### **The Many Morals of AI: The cautionary bias of North American news narratives in global perspective**
2610.18573v1 by Andrew Piper, Edie Pearman

The stories told about artificial intelligence (AI) play a central role in shaping public understanding, policy debates, and expectations about technological change. News media are especially influential in this process. Using a cross-regional corpus of 12,924 English-language news articles about AI published since 2022, we examine how storytelling about AI varies across media systems in four regions: North America, China, India, and the Middle East. Leveraging large language models, we extract the implicit narrative lessons conveyed by each article---commonly known as story morals---capturing value-laden interpretations that extend beyond thematic content or sentiment. We find that North American AI coverage is uniquely characterized by narratives of caution, while under-emphasizing narratives of innovation and collaboration that are more prevalent elsewhere. However, this pattern is largely explained by a broader regional negativity bias in North American news sentiment, rather than by heightened concern about AI itself. Our findings bring into view the culturally conditioned nature of AI news narratives.

摘要：

##### **Lost in Implicit Routing: How LLM Agents Fail to Load Relevant Skills at Scale**
2610.18522v1 by Jinlin Liu, Wei Chen, Xiaojin Zhang

Reusable skills let LLM agents load task-specific instructions on demand, but they make execution depend on implicit routing over skill metadata. We test whether this design scales by holding SkillsBench tasks and their golden skills fixed while adding irrelevant skills. We find skill routing collapse: agents increasingly load fewer or no skills, even though the relevant skills remain available. With 1,000 irrelevant skills, loaded-skill recall falls from 73.6% to 27.4% and the zero-load rate rises from 9.3% to 51.6%, whereas explicit selection from the same library retains 73.9% recall. Loading other skills, including plausible alternatives to golden skills, is too rare to explain this decline. Masking only the descriptions of irrelevant skills largely restores skill loading, yet with the metadata intact, explicitly asking agents to plan skill use still yields lower recall than preselection. These findings point to semantic interference from descriptions and instruction interference when skill choice competes with task execution. We also evaluate explicit preselection as a practical mitigation, including its coverage and latency trade-offs. Our analysis informs skill protocols and evaluation for agents with growing skill libraries.

摘要：

##### **The Geometry of Language Model Logits: Generic Structure and Learned Departures**
2610.18520v1 by Swathi Shree Narashiman, Aravind Ramana Venkatramanan, Nambi Seshadri

Autoregressive language models produce, at every prediction step, a vocabulary-sized logit vector whose structure determines the conditional next-token distribution. We empirically identify that the ordered vocabulary-head logits follow a remarkably consistent log-linear relation
  \[ z_{(r)} \approx μ- α\log r. \] across prediction contexts, architectures, and scales, emerging progressively during pretraining. To our knowledge, this is the first systematic analysis of the logit geometry underlying the distributional assumptions used in the prior decoding work. While controlled order-statistic baselines spanning bounded, light-tailed, and heavier-tailed distributions can largely explain the observed log-linear geometry, they do not explain the systematic shift in the slope $α$: trained language models exhibit $α\approx 1.4$--$1.7$, sitting well above the initialization baseline ($α\approx 0.2$) . Moreover, $α$ correlates with predictive performance, suggesting that it captures learned structure beyond generic extreme-value effects.

摘要：

##### **Efficient Safety Guardrails through Latent Dual-Hypothesis Reasoning with On-Demand Trace Reconstruction**
2610.18304v1 by Md Asiful Islam, Fahmida Alam, Mihai Surdeanu

Guardrail approaches that rely on reasoning-based large language models (LLM) improve safety decisions, but come with substantial inference overhead due to the explicit reasoning. We introduce LR-Guard (Latent Reasoning Guardrail), an efficient and explainable guardrail that jointly learns structured latent reasoning and on-demand reasoning reconstruction. Our method distills a frozen teacher's analysis of safe and unsafe prompt interpretations and their comparison into compact latent representations. Joint training optimizes these representations for both safety prediction and reconstruction of the teacher's explicit reasoning traces, enabling human-readable explanations without requiring intermediate text generation for every decision. Experiments show that LR-Guard outperforms its explicit-reasoning teacher in most in-domain and out-of-domain settings on safety classification and explanation extraction, while achieving a 19.4x inference speedup at batch size one. Further, reconstructed reasoning traces from the latent information exhibit high semantic similarity to the teacher's explicit reasoning.

摘要：

##### **Chain of Thought Monitoring Can Fail Even When CoT Is Necessary**
2610.18105v1 by Lennart Bürger, Piotr Komorowski, Moritz Weckbecker, Evgenii Kortukov, Aakriti Jain, Sebastian Lapuschkin, Wojciech Samek

Chain of Thought (CoT) monitoring is used by frontier labs to detect misbehaviour of AI systems. Yet, its reliability remains poorly understood. We attribute this to the lack of a successful organizing principle that leads to generalizable conclusions about monitoring reliability in different regimes. Recent work attributes monitoring failures to the CoT not being necessary for the answer, and argues that CoT becomes monitorable when it is necessary. We show that this binary necessary/not-necessary organizing principle is too coarse by demonstrating that monitoring failures persist even if the CoT is necessary for the answer. In response, we introduce a more fine-grained organizing principle based on a hierarchy of four CoT necessity levels. Building on this hierarchy, we develop a framework for measuring CoT monitorability at the three lowest levels via intervention experiments. We demonstrate monitoring failures at all three of these levels on realistic, hard prediction tasks. On a difficult medical-diagnosis task, patient sex and a clinical attribute influence the answer in at least 19% and 24% of correct responses with a necessary CoT, respectively, yet their influence is almost never articulated. These results show that even when CoT is necessary, it is not guaranteed to be monitorable.

摘要：

##### **inLoRA-MINT: Internal Representations for Auditing Training Data in Domain-adapted LLMs**
2610.18051v1 by Gonzalo Mancera, Julian Fierrez, Aythami Morales, Ruben Tolosana, Daniel DeAlcala, Francisco Jurado

This work investigates the application of the Membership Inference Test (MINT) to large language models (LLMs) fine-tuned with LoRA, aiming to determine whether specific samples were used during training and address concerns about data privacy, leakage, and transparency. Building on our previous LoRA-MINT framework, which relies exclusively on perplexity under a strict black-box setting, we propose an approach based on intermediate model representations, including activations and parameter-dependent signals. These representations enable a MINT classifier to distinguish training from non-training samples without requiring full access to model parameters.
  We evaluated the method on four LLMs (Qwen, Phi-4, DeepSeek-V3, and LLaMA 3.2-3B) fine-tuned on three datasets covering medical, mathematical, and academic reasoning tasks. Results show consistent performance across architectures and domains, achieving scores between 95% and 98%, with robustness to variations in model configurations and data distributions. The proposed approach enables efficient auditing of sensitive training data and supports data provenance and compliance with emerging privacy and AI governance regulations. Although our experiments focus on LoRA-based fine-tuning, the methodology can be extended to other LLM adaptation techniques and, more broadly, to other AI models.

摘要：

##### **Beyond Black Boxes: Visual Grounding for Text Embeddings**
2610.18047v1 by Slimane Larabi

In this paper, our goal is to represent text embeddings as sketches for three main reasons. First, to visualize text representation and to explore their potential their potential for AI explainability. Second, to investigate the potential benefits of the sketch-based representation for information retrieval. Third, to explore their potential for reasoning, drawing inspiration from the concept of mental imagery. We propose a sketch-based visual grounding framework that transforms text representations into visual topographic sketches. Our approach aims to emphasis the semantics content encoded in the text embeddings through the corresponding sketch.
  Evaluations are conducted on the MS-COCO dataset to examine the utility of these sketches for text retrieval. The comparative study of SBERT and Sketch-based method reveals that SBERT retains an advantage in strict top-1 retrieval accuracy while achieving comparable performance to the sketch-based method in top-5 retrieval. A cascaded approach combining SBERT and the sketch-based method outperforms SBERT alone, suggesting that the Sketch-based representation could offer significant potential for information retrieval if trained directly on text.

摘要：

##### **SHELDON: Reasoning through Dual Search over Hypothesis and Observation Spaces**
2610.18025v1 by Harshit Joshi, Priyank Shethia, Monica S. Lam

Test-time scaling has enabled LLMs to solve increasingly complex reasoning, discovery, and optimization tasks. However, existing approaches often gather observations while seeking evidence for current hypotheses, then generate new hypotheses conditioned on those hypotheses and their associated evidence. This couples observation discovery to existing hypotheses, favoring local refinements over exploration of unknown hypotheses regions. Inspired by the hypothetico-deductive method (Hempel, 1945) and dual-space models of scientific discovery (Klahr & Dunbar, 1988), we introduce SHELDON, a scientific reasoning harness that separately represents and jointly explores hypothesis and observation spaces. By actively seeking new observations along with new hypotheses, SHELDON can discover observations that its current hypotheses do not explain. These unexplained observations expose gaps in its understanding, motivate new investigations, and guide the generation of hypotheses beyond refinements of existing ones. Across diagnosis, abstract reasoning, and optimization benchmarks, SHELDON outperforms evaluated baselines by up to 26.7 points. Additional analysis shows that SHELDON generates up to 4.5x more novel solutions.

摘要：

##### **AI-Enhanced Hyperspectral Imaging for Intraoperative Detection of Low-Grade Glioma: A Clinical Study**
2610.17944v1 by Siri Willems, Ishika Jain, Jens De Winne, Siri Luthman, Hamed Zivari Adab, Roeland Vandebriel, Anna Alperovich, Xiaohan Zhang, Ben Stoffelen, Tom De Schepper, Lien Solie, Steven De Vleeschouwer

Current image-guided neuronavigation techniques, such as intraoperative MRI and ultrasound, are limited by high costs, limited sensitivity and operational complexity. There is a clear need for more efficient systems to enhance intra-operative tumor detection and improve patient outcomes for intrinsic brain tumor resection. Whereas previous intraoperative brain HSI studies have predominantly investigated high-grade gliomas, glioblastomas, or mixed-grade cohorts, this clinical study focuses specifically on low-grade gliomas (LGGs). LGGs strongly resemble normal brain under white light conditions on the operative microscope. This study characterizes spectral differences between healthy and tumor tissue and provides a paired comparison of HSI- and RGB-based classification at matched tissue locations and under identical patient-level data splits. Tumor regions showed consistently lower reflectance, which may be associated with differences in hemoglobin concentration and oxygenation. A range of classification models was evaluated. The 3D CNN, which integrates spectral and spatial features, achieved the highest median ROC-AUC of 74.5%, compared with 67.9% for the spectral-only XGBoost model and 64.0% for the RGB-based model. These results support further investigation of HSI for low-grade glioma classification and quantify its performance relative to conventional RGB imaging in this cohort.

摘要：

##### **Evaluating the Causal Faithfulness of SHAP as a Candidate Attribution Method for SAE Features**
2610.17898v1 by Samuel Xie, Noura Al-Moubayed

Sparse autoencoders decompose language model representations into interpretable features, but feature interpretation in this literature remains predominantly correlational. Attribution methods offer more direct estimates of feature influence, yet their faithfulness to actual causal effects has not been systematically evaluated in the SAE setting. We apply SHAP-based attribution to SAE latent features and evaluate it against direct causal intervention, using sentiment classification on SST-2 with GPT-2 Small and a 32,768-feature layer-7 SAE as a testbed. We compare SHAP to Integrated Gradients, Gradient$\times$Input, and probe-weight baselines across linear and MLP probe arms, scoring each against feature ablation and activation steering measured through the language model's own logit difference. Most faithfulness associations we measure do not survive multiple-comparison correction, and attribution methods that agree strongly with one another ($ρ= 0.42$--$0.80$) agree poorly with measured causal effects, indicating that inter-method agreement is not evidence of causal faithfulness. Two effects hold under control. First, local per-example attribution retains a directional advantage over global averaged attribution, while its apparent importance advantage is explained by feature activity rather than superior causal ranking. Second, under known-ground-truth minimal pairs, attribution recovers inserted sentiment signal well above chance (AUROC $0.792$) but fails to localize deletions (AUROC $0.520$), suggesting that these methods detect the presence of a feature's influence more reliably than its absence. We argue that importance faithfulness and directional faithfulness are separable properties requiring separate evaluation, and that attribution agreement should not substitute for causal validation.

摘要：

##### **A Tutorial on Quantum Amplitude Embedding with Data Re-Uploading**
2610.17836v1 by Hyojun Ahn, Joongheon Kim

Amplitude embedding lets a variational quantum model read many classical features with few qubits, but the model output is then only a quadratic form in the normalized input. Data re-uploading, which encodes the input repeatedly between trainable blocks, can be used to enrich such a model. Because every additional upload of an amplitude embedding repeats a costly state preparation, the gain that an upload buys has to be known before the gates are spent. This tutorial thus addresses the problem of explaining what data re-uploading adds to an amplitude-embedded model and how large the gain is. We express sequential re-uploading and parallel re-uploading with two copies in one notation and show that each upload raises the reachable polynomial order by two. Under one trainable circuit family and one budget of trainable parameters, simulation results show that re-uploading raises the test coefficient of determination from 0.689 to between 0.883 and 0.975 on targets of order four and from 0.337 to between 0.642 and 0.783 on targets of order six, on all ten seeds. On a quadratic target and on handwritten digits, one upload remains the best choice, and every additional upload lowers the gate error that the classifier tolerates. These results help designers match the number of uploads to the polynomial order of the task at hand.

摘要：

##### **Understanding Gene Expression-to-Image Learning: Spatial Structure and Information Fidelity**
2610.17699v1 by Xiaoqing Lian, Pei Liu, Jiahui Tan, Quan Zou, Chen Lin, Xiangxiang Zeng

Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) enables cell-level analysis of biological heterogeneity, but learning effective representations from high-dimensional, unordered gene expression profiles remains challenging. Gene Expression-to-Image (GE2I) offers an effective learning strategy by arranging gene values on pixel grids for convolutional neural networks, but its underlying mechanisms remain unclear. We study GE2I on full-transcriptome data while keeping the network and training procedure fixed. Our results reveal a trade-off between spatial smoothness and gene-level fidelity. Structured mappings produce smooth, low-frequency patterns that support faster early optimization, whereas assigning multiple genes to the same pixel obscures expression differences and can reduce predictive performance. This trade-off explains why random mappings can compete with structured layouts when many genes are represented. One-to-one gene-to-pixel assignment improves prediction across the tested visual backbones even when image smoothness decreases. We further find that the benefits of pretraining and augmentation depend on the backbone and mapping. These findings provide practical guidance for pixel allocation and the transfer of vision priors to gene expression data.

摘要：

##### **Assessing the Performance of AI Models in Misinformation Detection Using Predefined Social Noise Constructs**
2610.17469v1 by Manar Alsaid

Social media platforms generate vast volumes of user-generated content that often obscure meaningful discourse, a phenomenon conceptualized as social noise. While existing misinformation detection methods primarily focus on factual verification, they largely overlook the role of social noise in amplifying misinformation. This study assesses the performance of six text-based models and a graph neural network (GNN) in mitigating the effects of misinformation using predefined social noise constructs. The dataset consists of 500,000 tweets collected from the X platform using the Ukraine hashtag during the Russia-Ukraine conflict from March 2024 to March 2025. Advanced machine learning models achieve strong performance, with accuracies ranging from 79.94% to 97.17%. RoBERTa demonstrates the highest performance, with an accuracy of 97.17%, Cohen's kappa of 0.9660, MCC of 0.9660, and AUC of 0.9977. The GNN achieves an accuracy of 64.52% and an AUC of 0.899, demonstrating performance above random-chance classification by exploiting the relational structure between tweets. All text-based models achieve kappa values greater than 0.75, indicating substantial overall agreement. Explainable AI analysis using LIME reveals that 89.6% of predictive weight is attributed to contextual features rather than isolated keywords. The findings highlight that integrating social noise into AI pipelines enhances misinformation detection and supports more effective mitigation strategies in large-scale social media environments.

摘要：

##### **The belief-action gap in language model agents**
2610.17444v1 by Ayse Sila Okcu, Weisheng Wang, Tongmeng Xie, Niall Dalton, Angelos Nalmpantis, Raghu Arghal, Mario Giulianelli

Language model (LM) agents can fail for different reasons, requiring different explanations and interventions. We study how state beliefs, action values, and action preferences interact as LM agents reason and commit to decisions in text-rendered grid worlds. We estimate task-relevant beliefs using report-based and representation-based probes, and action values using inverse reinforcement learning. We find that task-relevant state and value information is often represented more reliably than it is reflected in behaviour. Tracking action preferences throughout reasoning further shows that decisions sharpen around a commitment point and reported belief uncertainty decreases near commitment, although individual belief changes do not consistently explain shifts in action preference. Finally, forced actions follow reasoning prefixes from other decisions when transplanted, whereas the tested reasoning-window activation edits provide limited control over the action. Together, these results suggest that some failures arise not from missing task-relevant information, but from how available information is translated into action. Our measurements distinguish information availability from belief-action consistency without by themselves identifying the cause of each failure. Our code is available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/belief-action-gap-F745/

##### **Learning to Detect Shifts in Unstructured Data Streams via Monitoring-by-Betting**
2610.17399v1 by Siyu Zhou, Jialin Ouyang, Fan Xia, Adarsh Subbaswamy, Drew Prinster, Suchi Saria, Augusto Garcia-Agundez, Sarah Pollet, Jinoos Yazdany, Patrick Vossler, Jean Feng

Continuous monitoring of generative AI systems requires detecting distribution shifts in the unstructured data they ingest and generate. Existing monitoring methods typically control false alarms by requiring the discrepancy measure used to detect shifts to be *pre-specified*. However, for unstructured data, pre-specification creates a sensitivity--breadth trade-off: a narrowly defined measure is sensitive to the anticipated shift but may miss unexpected changes, whereas a broadly defined measure may detect a wider range of shifts but with reduced sensitivity. Although adaptively hypothesizing the shift direction can overcome this limitation, such data-dependent adaptation generally invalidates conventional false-alarm guarantees. We reconcile adaptivity with statistical validity through testing-by-betting, reframing sequential changepoint detection as a betting game in which the bettor continually updates its hypothesized shift direction, or witness function, as new observations arrive. We introduce *asymptotic e-detectors*, which approximate the pre-change distribution using a calibration set of size $m$, and prove that they asymptotically control the average run length to a false alarm as $m$ increases. Empirically, calibration sets as small as $m=30$ provide effective control. We then develop adaptive monitoring strategies ranging from black-box bettors that learn from embeddings to interpretable bettors based on LLM-proposed concepts. Across three real-world datasets, including data from a clinical AI scribe deployment, adaptive monitors detect shifts faster than fixed monitors at the same false-alarm level. Further, when the true shifts are sparse and interpretable, LLM-concept monitors provide the fastest and most interpretable detection, while also guiding root-cause diagnosis for complex shifts.

##### **Learning to Play: Reinforcement Learning and Games**
2610.17391v1 by Aske Plaat

Amazing breakthroughs in reinforcement learning have taken place. Computers teach themselves to play Chess and Go and beat world champions. There is talk about expanding application areas towards general artificial intelligence (AI). The breakthroughs in Backgammon, Checkers, Chess, Atari, Go, Poker, and StarCraft have shown that we can build machines that exhibit intelligent game playing of the highest level. These successes have been widely publicized in the media, and inspire AI entrepreneurs and scientists alike. Reinforcement learning in games has become a mainstream AI research topic. It is a broad topic, and the successes build on a range of diverse techniques, from exact planning algorithms, to adaptive sampling, deep function approximation, and ingenious self-play methods. Perhaps because of the breadth of these technologies, or because of the recency of the breakthroughs, there are few books that explain these methods in depth. This book covers all methods in one comprehensive volume, explaining the latest research, bringing you as close as possible to working implementations, with many references to the original research papers. The programming examples in this book are in Python, the language in which most current reinforcement learning research is conducted. We help you to get started with machine learning frameworks such as Gym, TensorFlow, and Keras, and provide exercises to help understand how AI is learning to play.

##### **Does a Governed Confidence Score Know When a Text-to-SQL Answer Is Right? An Exploratory Study on BIRD and a Pre-registered Confirmation on Spider**
2610.17256v1 by Raghu Venkat, Tricha Anjali

Conversational analytics lets people ask questions of records spread across many systems and get answers in plain language; it is useful only if each answer can be trusted and explained. A production system of this kind attaches a confidence score to every answer it writes over a database and uses it to show the answer, show it with a warning, or withhold it. The score's weights, caps and thresholds were set by design. This paper audits that design. On the 1,534 questions of BIRD dev, answered by GPT-5.1 with a benchmark-adapted production prompt and five samples per question, the designed score separates right from wrong answers weakly (AUROC 0.624, 95% CI 0.595-0.652) and withholds 35.7% of correct answers. Nearly all of its ranking signal comes from agreement among the samples, and most of it is lost where agreement is combined by a minimum with a checklist that drops to zero on any unsupported claim, including claims in commentary. We froze a rule in which agreement with the shown answer is the score and deterministic checks act as vetoes and caps, and pre-registered a test on Spider dev before any Spider answer existed. The primary hypothesis held: AUROC rose from 0.566 to 0.725 (paired difference +0.159, 95% CI +0.097 to +0.221), and correct answers withheld fell from 49.0% to 8.8% with PASS accuracy 95.7%. Under one matching policy the choice of agreement statistic matters little; the gain comes from how agreement enters the score. Whether added checks help depends on how correctness is defined, while they always cost unqualified coverage. A pre-registered empty-result check did not meet its effect-size criterion on Spider. Correctness here means the core answer; labels are model-judged, and a blinded two-person audit, not yet complete, will be reported in a revised version.

##### **ChemExplain: A Multimodal Benchmark for Explanatory Chemical Reasoning**
2610.17183v1 by Aotian Chen, Frazier N. Baker, Reza Averly, Shan Zhang, Tianao Chen, Daniel Adu-Ampratwum, Xia Ning, Yuan Xue

Current chemistry benchmarks primarily evaluate outcome prediction or reaction pathway generation, but rarely test whether a model can explain why an observed chemical effect occurs. We introduce ChemExplain, a benchmark of 1,180 multiple-choice chemistry questions drawn from 383 peer-reviewed papers. Each question pairs a molecular or reaction image with an observed chemical effect and four candidate explanations, each associated with a different structural feature visible in the image. Answering requires the model to perceive the depicted structure, apply chemical knowledge, and identify which feature most directly explains the effect, a capability we term explanatory attribution. The correct feature-mechanism relation is derived from supporting evidence in the source paper rather than invented by the question-generation LLM. We construct the benchmark automatically with a multi-agent framework that applies progressive quality control at three stages and validate it with eight chemistry domain experts. The strongest evaluated model reaches 78.6% accuracy, leaving considerable room for improvement. On a 600-question expert-reviewed subset, reasoning-trace analysis classifies 71.4% of the strongest model's errors as attribution failures. Attribution is also the largest error category for the open-source and chemistry-specific models included in our analysis.

##### **Audit tools of the trade: Automated Auditor Elicitation Can Be Sensitive to the Context the Auditor Is Given**
2610.16911v1 by Ben Slater, Ryan Lundqvist, Leo Richter, Kevin Wei

Does providing additional context to automated AI auditors improve their effectiveness? Using the Petri tool to audit LLM coding agents, we test the impact of providing two types of resources (an external codebase and benchmark transcripts) to LLM auditors, across four auditor-target LLM pairs and three behaviours (harmful compliance, reward hacking, sycophancy). (1) External codebase: We provide the auditor LLM with a fixed, external open source codebase, which prior work finds improves the realism of synthetic audits. Providing this resource suppresses reward hacking in both auditor-target pairs whose baseline rate is above the floor (4.8 to 1.3 and 3.9 to 1.8 on Petri's default 1-10 scale); the remaining two pairs sit at the floor throughout. The suppression recurs in three of four pairs under a different scenario and resource bundle. (2) External benchmark transcripts: We provide the auditor with transcripts of the target's performance on benchmarks measuring similar behaviours, intended to inform the auditor about the target's behavioural patterns. Providing this resource raises reward hacking scores in all four pairs (from 1.7 to 6.1 in one case). However, subsequent experiments show that supplying only the related benchmark's abstract, without transcripts, also increases reward hacking, and the effect is absent under a different framing of the same behaviour. Overall, we find no consistent effect of either resource outside reward hacking, but we find substantial variability in judge-scored behaviour across conditions. Our results underscore the importance of careful piloting with tools such as Petri, as the measured rate of target behaviour is highly sensitive to configuration decisions. We hope our results inspire more research into the reliability and robustness of automated alignment auditing.

##### **Integrating Feature Understandability into Feature Selection for Human-Centred Tabular Explanations**
2610.16627v1 by Nicola Rossberg, Bennett Kleinberg, Barry O'Sullivan, Luca Longo, Andrea Visentin

Existing human-centred XAI research predominantly evaluates explanations after they have been generated. However, this fails to provide actionable insights into improving accessibility. This study incorporates human-based feature understandability into feature selection to improve the generation of human-centred explanations. Natural language explanations for machine learning models trained on tabular data often cite influential features in the input data to explain a given decision. As a consequence, users' ability to understand the produced explanation hinges on their understanding of the cited features. To quantify this natural-language feature understandability, Rossberg et al. proposed the Feature Understandability Scale, which assigns numerical `understandability scores' to features based on user feedback. To evaluate the degree to which these scores can improve machine learning explanation quality, this study collects understandability scores across two datasets, proposes an understandability-aware feature selection algorithm to select features that are both understandable and informative, and trains a random forest based on these feature subsets. The SHAP-based explanations generated by this model are evaluated using user feedback, and changes in explanation quality are assessed. The individual evaluation of the explanations showed that while the understandability-based explanations scored higher on average, the difference was not statistically significant. In contrast, the comparative evaluation found that participants preferred these explanations significantly more frequently than expected at the chance level. It is concluded that the integration of understandability can yield more understandable explanations at minimal cost to classification performance, although changes to quality require elucidation in larger cohorts.

##### **AI Discovery in Combinatorics: From Problems to Solutions and Back to Bigger Problems**
2610.16555v1 by Eugenio Cainelli, Lorenzo Luccioli, Alessandro Iraci, Giovanni Paolini

We introduce $qt$Bench, a benchmark of 29 open problems in algebraic combinatorics. These problems ask for a simple combinatorial algorithm that explains given polynomial coefficients or identities. To help AI agents search for such algorithms, we provide a scaffold for playing with combinatorial objects, countering cheating attempts, and testing candidate solutions. To explore how far frontier AI systems can go on these problems, we collaborated with OpenAI, whose researchers obtained proposed solutions to several of them. This highlights bigger meta-problems: is AI discovery at scale ethical? How should these results be released? How should proof digestion be organized? We discuss these questions and give our views.

##### **pi-Net: Empirical Latent Representations for Differentiable Physics Heads in Data-Scarce Systems**
2610.16504v1 by Akshay Dhalpe, Arnab Chattopadhyay, Shahriar Bakrani Balani, Eric Cortanéa, Sasu Tarkoma

Developing reliable predictive models for multi-physics systems often forces a compromise between computationally demanding physical simulators and data-hungry, opaque machine learning (ML) algorithms. While incorporating dimensional analysis into directed computation graphs offers a structurally sound alternative to explicitly solving partial differential equations (PDEs), such formulations typically yield underdetermined systems that act as imperfect simulators. We introduce pi-Net, a physics-informed latent variable model that resolves these imperfect simulators. The hybrid pi-Net architecture consists of an encoder that maps inputs into physically normalized latent space, which are then decoded into final predictions by a differentiable physics head. Evaluated on a real-world, data-scarce Gas Metal Arc Welding (GMAW) case study, the framework achieves an R2 of 0.864 with only 60 experimental samples. The results demonstrate that pi-Net significantly outperforms purely data-driven baselines in data-scarce regimes while providing causal, process-level explainability.

##### **XAI-Guided Fourier Domain Generalisation for Pure-Simulation Sim-to-Real Deep Reinforcement Learning**
2610.16478v1 by Wei Yao, Chuyao Wang, Ziwei Wang, Nabil Aouf

Deep reinforcement learning has shown strong potential for vision-based autonomous driving, but policies trained in simulation often suffer severe performance degradation when deployed in the real world. Existing sim-to-real methods commonly address this gap through image-transfer-based domain adaptation or policy-agnostic domain randomisation. However, the former requires laborious acquisition of target-domain data and is often limited to the deployment domain, whereas the latter introduces unguided perturbations that may not reflect the underlying sim-to-real discrepancy, leading to suboptimal transfer performance. To overcome these limitations, we propose XFD, an explainable artificial intelligence (XAI)-guided Fourier domain generalisation framework for pure-simulation sim-to-real reinforcement learning. The proposed framework extracts explainability attribution maps from simulated observations, and converts these attributions into policy-conditioned Fourier-domain interventions. By perturbing appearance-sensitive spectral components while preserving task-defining spatial structures, XFD constructs robustness-enhancing visual domains entirely within simulation. Subsequently, the proposed robust policy optimisation guides the policy towards attending to task-critical features while suppressing sensitivity to irrelevant visual information. Extensive experiments in simulation and zero-shot real-world deployment demonstrate that XFD consistently improves traveled distance and reward over standard policy and randomly domain-randomised baseline, especially on unseen maps with cluttered backgrounds. Attribution-based analysis further proves that the proposed policy preserves more consistent task-relevant visual evidence across simulation and real-world observations.

##### **VisExMEM: Visual Evidence-Based Explainable Multimodal Evaluation Metric**
2610.16441v1 by Subhadeep Roy, Christoph Leiter, Steffen Eger

Current image-text evaluation often relies on a global score, which gives limited insight into which semantic details are supported by the image. Explainable metrics provide rationales, but the visual evidence supporting those rationales can still be difficult to verify. We introduce VisExMEM, a reference-free evaluator that decomposes captions into atomic claims, localizes visual evidence, verifies each claim, and averages claim-level support into a global score. Explanations use the same evidence and decisions. We train Qwen3.5-based 4B and 9B models on 100K samples with multi-task SFT, counterfactual refinement, and evidence-aware GDPO. Across eight benchmarks, VisExMEM-9B achieves the highest descriptive composite among open-source evaluators in our comparison, exceeding VQAScore by 11.7 points while remaining competitive with GPT-5.6 Sol. We also introduce AGITA-Bench, containing 600 human-annotated image-text pairs and 8.7K atomic claims for global and claim-level evaluation. For untuned Qwen3.5 and GPT-5.6 Sol controls, decomposed prompting raises the same composite by 3.6-5.1 points relative to holistic prompting. Human evaluation shows that VisExMEM explanations outperform prior explainable metrics and approach GPT-5.6 Sol.

##### **Where Polanyi Meets Galois: Tacit Knowing as an Algebro-Deterministic Operation, Toward Re-rooting AI-Generated Science**
2610.16102v1 by Hiroyuki Chuma, Kanji Otsuka, Yoichi Sato, Kodo Yamauchi

We argue that tacit knowing is better modelled as an operating regime of one memory substrate than as a kind of content. The substrate is VaCoAl, an algebro-deterministic XOR-and-shift hyperdimensional memory over GF(2), realized as CASRAM (commercial SRAM-CAM) and PyVaCoAl (DRAM-CAM).In VaCoAl/PyVaCoAl a directed acyclic graph (DAG) reads two ways. Repairing every collision (Rescue mode) makes the trace exactly auditable but ranks no path higher; tolerating collisions (Don't Care mode) yields a path-integrated confidence, CR$_2$, that silently favours direct routes. This audit-valuation complementarity is a formal counterpart of Polanyi's claim that tacit and explicit knowledge are not sharply divided. It separates contingent tacitness, which a new query brings out (AlphaGo's game tree), from regime-bound tacitness, statable only in a regime where it is gone (AlphaGo's hunches). (1) Polanyi's from-to structure reads as non-commutative binding, the Identikit as role-filler composition, recognition as majority-vote coincidence. (2) Explicating the valuation destroys it, as attending to his fingers can make Polanyi's pianist falter, yet a closed-form law states it. Polanyi, Nonaka, Tsoukas and Collins each capture part of this but narrowly; even Nonaka and von Krogh's continuum cannot tell contingent from regime-bound tacitness. (3) Collins's three tacit knowledges become strata, one outside the substrate. (4) Our mentor-student DAG is a tacit-transmission graph. (5) Terence Tao's remark that AI-solved problems spawn few follow-ups is diagnosed as rootless explicit knowledge: checkable results whose route no one can follow and that no one yet understands well enough to extend, teach or judge. To re-root them, LLMs generate, VaCoAl/PyVaCoAl keeps an auditable trace with its confidence, and people value, a role virtue epistemology shows cannot be offloaded. Testable predictions follow.

##### **The Sharp Directions Are Against You: Curvature Analysis of Activation Steering**
2610.16084v1 by Seyedarmin Azizi, Arya Fayyazi, Parsa Razmara, Erfan Baghaei Potraghloo, Souvik Kundu, Massoud Pedram

Activation steering adds a vector to a language model's internal representations at inference time. It is a lightweight alternative to fine-tuning for behavioral control. The standard construction, contrastive activation addition (CAA), is fragile, as on many inputs it shifts behavior in the wrong direction. To explain this fragility, we first study the geometry of the steering vector. We introduce steering Hessian, the matrix of second order derivative of the model's behavioral loss with respect to the steering vector. It captures the output behavior response sensitivity of the model under small perturbations of the steering vector along each direction. Its top eigenvectors are sharp directions: tiny perturbations along them can cause large, erratic, input-dependent behavioral swings. Bottom eigenvectors are flat directions: behavior is robust along them. Removing the sharp component of a steering vector approximately preserves its direction and magnitude (cosine > 0.974, norm reduction < 3% for typical settings) while reducing the vector's directional curvature by up to 12x (median 4x across 36 conditions). We evaluate on three model families (Qwen2.5-7B, Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B), with four behavioral tasks, and 36 token-level conditions. Steering along sharp directions reverses the intended behavior in every condition tested, with anti-steer rates of 53-100%. Sharp directions account for only 1-5% of the CAA vector's squared norm. However, they drive a disproportionate share of the gap between the steered and unsteered next-token distributions during autoregressive generation. We term this one-line correction as sharp removal; it outperforms standard CAA in open-ended generation across all 25 conditions tested. A jailbreaking experiment further shows that sharp directions act as noise rather than signal: they disrupt coherent behavioral control regardless of steering polarity.

##### **AI Representations of Mental Health Stigma: Quantifying the Gap Between AI Estimations and Human Perceptions Across Gender Groups Using GPT-Based LLMs**
2610.16042v1 by Soojong Kim, Hye Min Kim, Kwanho Kim, Dongdong Zhu

The increasing use of artificial intelligence (AI) in mental health applications raises concerns about its potential to internalize and perpetuate societal biases. This research evaluates both disparities and misalignment in how large language models (LLMs), GPT-4 and GPT-5, represent perceptions of mental health across gender groups. The research comprises two studies. Study 1 applied widely adopted measures of Social Distance, Stereotypes, and Attitudes Toward Seeking Help to assess whether the AI models depict systematic gender differences. Study 2 compared AI estimations of Personal, Self, and Perceived Stigma with large-scale real-world human data from the Healthy Minds Study (N = 76,406) to evaluate alignment between AI estimations and human perceptions. Results from Study 1 showed the AI models consistently portrayed men as having the greatest desire for social distance from someone with mental health problems, relatively higher Stereotypes scores, and the least favorable attitudes toward help-seeking, compared with women and nonbinary individuals. Study 2 confirmed that this ordinal pattern partially aligned with human-reported data for Personal and Self Stigma. However, the AI models systematically underestimated Personal Stigma and Perceived Stigma while substantially overestimating Self Stigma for all gender groups. These findings reveal that, although the AI models have learned relative gender-based patterns reflecting human experiences to a certain extent, they function as distorted portrayals, highlighting the need for rigorous, context-specific validation against real-world data to ensure AI's safe and equitable deployment.

##### **RetryBench: Separating Retry Permission from Recovery Outcomes in GPT Tool Use**
2610.15884v1 by You Liu, Yue Liu, Quanchao Lu

False retry permission (FRP) occurs when an agent retries a timed-out tool call without support from the visible contract. RetryBench separates this error from realized recovery. A later 240-response control removed the candidate and required models to supply a key. Luna still made 8/80 unsupported dispatches; Sol and Terra made none in 80 responses each. These results support reporting permission and recovery together across declared interface variants. The evidence covers four purposive tasks, fixed GPT endpoints, and one recovery decision; it identifies neither internal mechanisms nor episode-level safety.

Second, we fine-tune four Urdu-capable transformers under a common grid-search protocol and compare them against twelve feature-classifier baselines on identical data partitions. The best configuration, Multilingual Representations for Indian Languages (MuRIL), reaches 87.05% accuracy and 87.06% macro F1, improving on the strongest baseline by 5.52 percentage points, a margin that exceeds the 95% confidence interval on test accuracy; the two leading transformers differ by less than the test set can resolve, and performance is stable across five optimizers. ##### **ClinEdge: An Expert-Graded Benchmark of Real Patient Edge Cases Where Frontier Multimodal Models Fail**
2610.15239v1 by Anurag Sharma, Aditya Sharma, Sapna Baalwan

Most benchmarks for large language models in medicine are built from licensing examinations and theoretical questions. The few that draw on real clinical data do so only in part, and rarely require a model to read a patient's own imaging. ClinEdge addresses this gap with 32 real, de-identified patient cases from active clinical practice, each evaluated across seven frontier systems for a total of 224 expert-graded evaluations. Each case pairs primary imaging with a short vignette, contains a feature known to defeat pattern-matched reasoning, and is scored against the patient's documented clinical course. The suite is built for depth rather than volume. Grading followed a two-stage expert process. From the experts' notes we catalogue 18 recurring failure modes in five families. The models stop reading an image at the first finding. They give generic plans and lean toward over-treatment. AI coding agents operate in iterative loops: they modify code, run tools, inspect the results, and decide what to do next. ##### **Iterate on an LLM, Serve on an SLM**
2610.14576v1 by Keegan Wang

At the same time, small language models (SLMs) also gain more capabilities but have largely been able to keep their cost stable, offering 5-40 times cheaper inference for the narrow, repetitive subtasks that agents decompose work into. Furthermore, when the two disagreed, a blinded professional scheduler sided with the student 48-29. ##### **Reels-RSI: Program-Level Self-Improvement for Code-Rendered Explainer Videos**
2610.14495v1 by Wei Lin

Explainer videos -- a worked geometry problem, why the sky is blue, how a dynasty fell -- are usually one more target for pixel video generation. We argue they are a different kind of artifact: a program (script, storyboard and per-frame animation code) that a deterministic renderer turns into frames. A program can be read, diffed, tested and audited; pixels can only be watched. Reels-RSI is an open-source reel director for any agent harness; its self-improvement happens at the program level rather than in weights or prompts. Every revision pass is logged with a code snapshot; a retrospective pairs each check finding with the diff that fixed it and proposes lessons; a lesson that a static check can catch is compiled into a rule with a must-fail and a must-pass example, so the memory is unit-tested; and a rule learned today runs over everything made before it. A bounded, benchmark-gated step lets an agent rewrite the director's playbook, kept only when blind pairwise judgments on held-out topics prefer it; nothing enters memory without a human decision. Across 111 reels -- a field study, two playbook evolutions, a 2x2 memory ablation, a weaker director, a 48-reel learning curve and a judge-reliability study -- we find that the rule learned in the field caught four latent defects in frames authored before it existed and none after; that rules are an audit for a strong director (eight unguarded reels, no violation) and a guard for a weaker one (thirty violations unguarded versus five); that lessons shorten revision loops but do not compound or move the judge; and that the first rewrite's 11--1 verdict was a position bias in our vote design (judges pick the second-shown reel 62--81% of the time), masking scripts that traded thirteen facts for craft. We document four failure modes of the preference signal, which check caught each, and the fixes. Code, rules, lessons and data are public.

2610.14474v1 by Cong Wang, Jin Li, Shoujin Wang, Yishuo Li, Huilin Gu, Wenpeng Lu

Medication recommendations seek to deliver personalized drug combinations based on patients' clinical information, where ensuring transparent decision-making and personalized safety are of paramount importance. This stage employs disentangled drug factors to construct patient-specific reference prototypes that capture desired therapeutic drug features and risk features to avoid. These prototypes enable risk boundary calibration and guide the model toward safe recommendations via reward shaping and clipping. ##### **Deep learning from a control vantage point**
2610.14385v1 by Álvaro Rodríguez Abella, João Pedro Silvestre, Bahman Gharesifard, Paulo Tabuada

This article offers an overview of recent developments in deep learning from a control vantage point. The article explains how residual neural networks can be interpreted as the discretization of a continuous-time control system thereby enabling the use of control theoretic techniques for its analysis. To illustrate the power of this bridge between control and deep learning, two technical results are discussed: 1) the ability of a residual neural network to approximate arbitrary continuous functions is a non-standard controllability problem that can be solved using geometric nonlinear control and 2) transformers, the key architectural component of most large language-models, can be seen the discretization of a consensus algorithm and thus large language-models exhibit consensus. Another point of contact between control theory and deep learning is the connection between backpropagation, the algorithm used to train neural networks, and optimal control. The article addresses how the older connection between these problems, established in the 80's using discrete-time models can be reconciled with the more recent approach using continuous-time models. Several open problems and directions for future research are discussed, including selective state-space models, a recent class of models inspired by the models used in control.

##### **Which Metadata Matters for LLM Tool Selection in MCP? Evidence from Controlled Factor-Level Interventions**
2610.14334v1 by Xiaofan Li, Fengchun Qiao, Xing Gao

In Model Context Protocol (MCP) systems, LLMs select tools from metadata rather than from their implementations. Names and descriptions summarize what a tool does, while the parameter schema specifies the fields a tool accepts and how they are organized. Our analysis of metadata for 367,174 MCP tools reveals substantial schema variation among functionally similar tools. Many of these tools share names and similar descriptions but expose different parameter schemas. This raises a key question: can parameter-schema differences influence tool selection before any call is made? We study this question through controlled metadata interventions across five LLMs and five tasks. Each comparison changes exactly one metadata factor (server name, tool name, description, or parameter schema) while keeping the others fixed. We balance tool positions and use conditional logistic regression to estimate position-adjusted effects. We find that: (1) parameter-schema changes produce the largest and most consistent selection shifts among the tested interventions. Models tend to favor separate top-level parameters over grouped or composite forms. (2) Descriptions that explain parameters also tend to increase selection, while name effects are weaker and less consistent. (3) Targeted comparisons show that schema effects depend on the specific parameter names and on how the request represents the same information. When only one schema satisfies an explicit request requirement, all five models select the compatible tool despite position preferences. These results show that parameter schemas influence not only how tools are called, but also whether they are selected. ##### **The RNN Homunculus: Learning to Gate Long-Term Memory**
2610.14279v1 by Zeyu Fan, Alexandru-Dragos Manolache, Jan van Gemert

Learning long-range dependencies with recurrent neural networks (RNNs) is difficult because of the vanishing gradient problem. Gated recurrent neural networks like GRUs and LSTMs address this problem and are commonly explained by their ability to create additive copy paths through time. This explanation is correct, but incomplete: useful RNN gate values must themselves be learned, which is typically done with another RNN. We study this gate-learning problem through carefully controlled sequence classification tasks. Our analysis shows that gated architectures struggle when all training samples require long-range memory and thus equally suffer from the vanishing gradient problem. We intervene by adding short-dependency samples which let gated models learn solutions that capture long-dependency samples, but also generalize across different class labels. When properly learned, gated RNNs match or even exceed the test accuracy of modern architectures like Mamba and xLSTM. Overall, our results highlight gate learning as a central part of learning long-range memory and show how training data can support this process by exposing the same task over shorter dependencies.

摘要：漫畫是二十世紀偉大的視覺記錄之一：數十萬頁中，大眾讀者看到了他們的領袖、戰爭和英雄。這一記錄的大部分從未作為整體進行過研究；漫畫學術研究通常是一本一本地進行。我們認為，人工智慧，包括生成模型，可以改變這一現狀，從而使媒介本身受益。我們提出了PAGE，一個將人工智慧作為漫畫研究工具的框架。它將每個掃描解析為面板、文本和書目記錄；用遵循書面代碼本的視覺-語言模型為每頁進行註釋，正如人類編碼者所做的那樣；將這些讀取樣本與人類註釋相結合；並解釋整個檔案中的變化，並對出版商和類型進行控制估算。我們在3929本美國漫畫（1938-1956年）和87卷日本漫畫上展示了該框架，進行了三項研究，每項研究基於不同的能力。第一項將文本搜索與視覺閱讀相結合，以恢復大眾媒介如何描繪政治權力。第二項測量整個行業的頁面視覺形式，並恢復個別藝術家的風格。第三項則追蹤在兩個漫畫傳統中誰在發言，並與人類註釋進行核對。除了研究，這些工具還可以使檔案根據所繪製的內容可搜索，讓盲人和視力低下的讀者接觸漫畫，並幫助保存脆弱的收藏，給未獲得認可的藝術家以信用。我們概述了一個研究議程，其中人工智慧加強了對這一媒介的研究、保存和影響力。
